React unmounts everything on an\n * uncaught render error, so without this a single throw anywhere — a menu part\n * used outside its required parent, a bad snapshot parse — blanks the window\n * with no clue as to why.\n *\n * Deliberately dependency-light: it must keep working when whatever it caught\n * has already broken the surrounding UI.\n */\nexport class AppErrorBoundary extends Component<AppErrorBoundaryProps, AppErrorBoundaryState> {\n  state: AppErrorBoundaryState = { error: null, componentStack: null };\n\n  static getDerivedStateFromError(error: Error): Partial<AppErrorBoundaryState> {\n    return { error };\n  }\n\n  componentDidCatch(error: Error, info: ErrorInfo) {\n    this.setState({ componentStack: info.componentStack ?? null });\n    console.error(\"[omniharness] uncaught render error\", error, info.componentStack);\n  }\n\n  private handleRetry = () => {\n    this.setState({ error: null, componentStack: null });\n  };\n\n  private handleReload = () => {\n    window.location.reload();\n  };\n\n  render() {\n    const { error, componentStack } = this.state;\n    if (!error) {\n      return this.props.children;\n    }\n\n    const details = [error.stack || `${error.name}: ${error.message}`, componentStack]\n      .filter(Boolean)\n      .join(\"\\n\\n\");\n\n    return (\n      <div role=\"alert\" className=\"flex min-h-screen items-center justify-center bg-background p-6\">\n        <div className=\"w-full max-w-2xl rounded-lg border border-destructive/30 bg-destructive/[0.03] p-5\">\n          <div className=\"flex items-start gap-3\">\n            <AlertTriangle className=\"mt-0.5 h-5 w-5 shrink-0 text-destructive\" />\n            <div className=\"min-w-0 flex-1\">\n              <h1 className=\"text-sm font-semibold text-foreground\">\n                {t(\"app.crash.title\")}\n              </h1>\n              <p className=\"mt-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground\">\n                {t(\"app.crash.description\")}\n              </p>\n              <p className=\"mt-3 break-words font-mono text-xs text-destructive\">\n                {error.message || String(error)}\n              </p>\n              {details ? (\n                <details className=\"mt-3\">\n                  <summary className=\"cursor-pointer text-xs text-muted-foreground\">\n                    {t(\"app.crash.details\")}\n                  </summary>\n                  <pre className=\"mt-2 max-h-64 overflow-auto whitespace-pre-wrap break-words rounded-md bg-muted/40 p-2 font-mono text-[10px] leading-4 text-muted-foreground\">\n                    {details}\n                  </pre>\n                </details>\n              ) : null}\n              <div className=\"mt-4 flex flex-wrap gap-2\">\n                <Button type=\"button\" size=\"sm\" onClick={this.handleReload}>\n                  {t(\"app.crash.reload\")}\n                </Button>\n                <Button type=\"button\" size=\"sm\" variant=\"outline\" onClick={this.handleRetry}>\n                  {t(\"app.crash.retry\")}\n                </Button>\n              </div>\n            </div>\n          </div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    );\n  }\n}\nimport fs from \"fs/promises\";\nimport path from \"path\";\nimport { resolveRestartControlConfig } from \"@/server/restart-control\";\n\n/**\n * A runner cannot restart itself: the process serving the request is the one\n * that has to die, so it could never send a response or bring itself back. The\n * restart-control server (a separate process, port 3099 by default) owns the\n * lifecycle for exactly that reason.\n *\n * So \"Restart server\" in the UI proxies to that control server. The browser\n * cannot call it directly — it is a different origin and its bearer token lives\n * in a 0600 file on disk — so the runner reads the token and forwards the call.\n */\nexport type RunnerRestartOutcome =\n  | { ok: true; pid: number | null; mode: string | null; startedAt: number | null }\n  | { ok: false; code: \"control_unavailable\" | \"control_unauthorized\" | \"control_failed\"; message: string };\n\nconst CONTROL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;\n\nasync function readRestartToken(repoRoot: string, configuredToken: string | null) {\n  if (configuredToken?.trim()) {\n    return configuredToken.trim();\n  }\n  try {\n    const raw = await fs.readFile(path.join(repoRoot, \".omniharness\", \"remote-restart-token\"), \"utf8\");\n    return raw.trim() || null;\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\nfunction isConnectionRefused(error: unknown) {\n  const chain: unknown[] = [error, (error as { cause?: unknown } | null)?.cause];\n  return chain.some((entry) => {\n    const code = (entry as { code?: unknown } | null)?.code;\n    const message = entry instanceof Error ? entry.message : \"\";\n    return code === \"ECONNREFUSED\" || /\\bECONNREFUSED\\b|\\bfetch failed\\b/i.test(message);\n  });\n}\n\nexport async function requestRunnerRestart(options: {\n  repoRoot?: string;\n  env?: Record<string, string | undefined>;\n  fetchImpl?: typeof fetch;\n} = {}): Promise<RunnerRestartOutcome> {\n  const repoRoot = options.repoRoot ?? process.cwd();\n  const env = options.env ?? process.env;\n  const doFetch = options.fetchImpl ?? fetch;\n  const config = resolveRestartControlConfig(repoRoot, env);\n  const token = await readRestartToken(repoRoot, config.token ?? null);\n\n  if (!token) {\n    return {\n      ok: false,\n      code: \"control_unavailable\",\n      message: \"The restart control service has no token on this machine. Start it with `pnpm restart:control`.\",\n    };\n  }\n\n  try {\n    const response = await doFetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${config.port}/restart-current`, {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      headers: {\n        Authorization: `Bearer ${token}`,\n        Accept: \"application/json\",\n      },\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(CONTROL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),\n    });\n\n    if (response.status === 401 || response.status === 403) {\n      return {\n        ok: false,\n        code: \"control_unauthorized\",\n        message: \"The restart control service rejected this runner's token.\",\n      };\n    }\n\n    if (!response.ok) {\n      const detail = await response.text().catch(() => \"\");\n      return {\n        ok: false,\n        code: \"control_failed\",\n        message: `The restart control service returned ${response.status}${detail ? `: ${detail.slice(0, 200)}` : \".\"}`,\n      };\n    }\n\n    const payload = await response.json().catch(() => ({})) as {\n      pid?: unknown;\n      mode?: unknown;\n      startedAt?: unknown;\n    };\n    return {\n      ok: true,\n      pid: typeof payload.pid === \"number\" ? payload.pid : null,\n      mode: typeof payload.mode === \"string\" ? payload.mode : null,\n      startedAt: typeof payload.startedAt === \"number\" ? payload.startedAt : null,\n    };\n  } catch (error) {\n    if (isConnectionRefused(error)) {\n      return {\n        ok: false,\n        code: \"control_unavailable\",\n        message: `No restart control service is listening on port ${config.port}. Start it with \\`pnpm restart:control\\`.`,\n      };\n    }\n    return {\n      ok: false,\n      code: \"control_failed\",\n      message: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),\n    };\n  }\n}\nimport { StateManager } from \"@/lib/state-manager\";\nimport type { MessageRecord } from \"./types\";\n\n/**\n * Single source of truth for messages this client has sent but not yet seen\n * come back on the event stream.\n *\n * This replaced three parallel refs (`pendingSentConversationMessagesRef`,\n * `locallySentMessageIdsRef`, `sendingMessageIdsRef`) that described one\n * lifecycle between them. Refs were the wrong home twice over: mutating a\n * `Set` in place never re-rendered the components reading it — the send-time\n * dimming cleared on whatever unrelated render happened next — and the three\n * could disagree about the same message.\n *\n * Lifecycle of one send:\n *   beginSend   POST issued; row rendered optimistically, still `inFlight`\n *   settleSend  POST returned; the row is real, but the stream has not\n *               carried it yet, so it stays as a re-injection guard\n *   failSend    POST rejected; drop the row\n *   acknowledge the stream carried it; the server copy takes over\n */\nexport type SentConversationMessageEntry = {\n  message: MessageRecord;\n  /**\n   * The POST has not settled. Such a row can never be stale, so the\n   * staleness checks that guard against resurrecting old rows must not\n   * apply to it.\n   */\n  inFlight: boolean;\n};\n\ntype SentConversationMessagesState = {\n  entries: ReadonlyMap<string, SentConversationMessageEntry>;\n};\n\nconst EMPTY_STATE: SentConversationMessagesState = { entries: new Map() };\n\nexport class SentConversationMessagesManager extends StateManager<SentConversationMessagesState> {\n  constructor() {\n    super(EMPTY_STATE);\n  }\n\n  private write(mutate: (draft: Map<string, SentConversationMessageEntry>) => boolean) {\n    return this.update((current) => {\n      const draft = new Map(current.entries);\n      return mutate(draft) ? { entries: draft } : current;\n    });\n  }\n\n  beginSend(message: MessageRecord) {\n    this.write((draft) => {\n      draft.set(message.id, { message, inFlight: true });\n      return true;\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * The POST settled. `message` is the persisted row, which normally carries\n   * the same id the optimistic row already used (the client supplies it as\n   * `clientMessageId`); a server that minted its own id instead is handled by\n   * dropping the optimistic entry and tracking the returned one. A send that\n   * produced no row — queued behind a busy worker — just clears.\n   */\n  settleSend(sentMessageId: string, message: MessageRecord | null | undefined) {\n    this.write((draft) => {\n      const had = draft.delete(sentMessageId);\n      if (message) {\n        draft.set(message.id, { message, inFlight: false });\n        return true;\n      }\n      return had;\n    });\n  }\n\n  failSend(sentMessageId: string) {\n    this.write((draft) => draft.delete(sentMessageId));\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * A row the server handed back outside the optimistic send path — a queued\n   * message released to the worker. There was no optimistic bubble to swap,\n   * but it still needs shadowing until the stream carries it.\n   */\n  trackDeliveredMessage(message: MessageRecord) {\n    this.write((draft) => {\n      draft.set(message.id, { message, inFlight: false });\n      return true;\n    });\n  }\n\n  /** The event stream now carries these rows; stop shadowing them. */\n  acknowledge(messageIds: readonly string[]) {\n    if (messageIds.length === 0) {\n      return;\n    }\n    this.write((draft) => messageIds.reduce(\n      (changed, messageId) => draft.delete(messageId) || changed,\n      false,\n    ));\n  }\n\n  getPendingMessages(): ReadonlyMap<string, MessageRecord> {\n    const pending = new Map<string, MessageRecord>();\n    for (const [messageId, entry] of this.getSnapshot().entries) {\n      pending.set(messageId, entry.message);\n    }\n    return pending;\n  }\n\n  getInFlightMessageIds(): ReadonlySet<string> {\n    const inFlight = new Set<string>();\n    for (const [messageId, entry] of this.getSnapshot().entries) {\n      if (entry.inFlight) {\n        inFlight.add(messageId);\n      }\n    }\n    return inFlight;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Every message this client sent this session. `Terminal` uses it to place\n   * a fallback row immediately instead of waiting to rule out that the row is\n   * hiding in an unfetched history page — a message we just sent cannot be.\n   */\n  getLocallySentMessageIds(): ReadonlySet<string> {\n    return new Set(this.getSnapshot().entries.keys());\n  }\n}\n\nexport const sentConversationMessagesManager = new SentConversationMessagesManager();\n/**\n * Recover a worker transcript whose on-disk stream lost its head.\n *\n * Symptom this repairs: `<runId>/workers/<workerId>.jsonl` starts at a seq\n * far above 1 while `artifact_streams.latest_seq` matches the tail. That\n * happens when the stream file is (re)created while the DB cursor is\n * already advanced — `refreshChainCaches` seeds `nextSeq` from\n * `max(fileMaxSeq, dbLatestSeq) + 1`, so the writer resumes numbering\n * mid-stream and never backfills the missing prefix.\n *\n * The raw bridge output survives in\n * `.omniharness/agent-runtime-output/<workerId>.jsonl`, which is the\n * authoritative chronological record. This script merges that archive with\n * the surviving stream tail, re-numbers the result from 1, and rewrites the\n * stream atomically under the worker file lock.\n *\n * Entries that only ever existed in the stream (`user_input`, `lifecycle` —\n * they are produced server-side, not by the bridge) are preserved and spliced\n * back in chronologically. `user_input` timestamps are corrected from the\n * `messages` table when a match exists, because the stream records the flush\n * time rather than the send time.\n *\n * Verification is the default; `--apply` is opt-in and always leaves a\n * `.pre-recovery-<ts>.bak` beside the rewritten file.\n *\n * Usage:\n *   pnpm exec tsx scripts/recover-worker-transcript.ts --runId e51514929b72\n *   pnpm exec tsx scripts/recover-worker-transcript.ts --runId e51514929b72 --apply\n *   pnpm exec tsx scripts/recover-worker-transcript.ts --all-damaged\n *   pnpm exec tsx scripts/recover-worker-transcript.ts --all-damaged --apply\n */\nimport { promises as fs, existsSync } from \"node:fs\";\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { randomUUID } from \"node:crypto\";\nimport { db } from \"@/server/db\";\nimport { artifactStreams, runs, workers, messages } from \"@/server/db/schema\";\nimport { eq, and } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { resolveArtifactStreamLocation } from \"@/server/artifacts/append-only-store\";\nimport { compactEntryForHistory } from \"@/server/workers/output-store\";\n\ninterface Entry {\n  id?: string;\n  type?: string;\n  text?: string;\n  timestamp?: string;\n  seq?: number;\n  raw?: unknown;\n  [key: string]: unknown;\n}\n\ninterface Options {\n  runIds: string[];\n  allDamaged: boolean;\n  apply: boolean;\n  force: boolean;\n  archiveDir: string;\n}\n\nfunction parseArgs(argv: string[]): Options {\n  const opts: Options = {\n    runIds: [],\n    allDamaged: false,\n    apply: false,\n    force: false,\n    archiveDir: path.resolve(process.cwd(), \".omniharness/agent-runtime-output\"),\n  };\n  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i += 1) {\n    const arg = argv[i];\n    if (arg === \"--apply\") opts.apply = true;\n    else if (arg === \"--force\") opts.force = true;\n    else if (arg === \"--all-damaged\") opts.allDamaged = true;\n    else if (arg === \"--runId\") opts.runIds.push(argv[++i] ?? \"\");\n    else if (arg === \"--archive-dir\") opts.archiveDir = path.resolve(argv[++i] ?? \"\");\n    else if (arg === \"--help\" || arg === \"-h\") {\n      console.log(\"Usage: recover-worker-transcript.ts [--runId <id>]... Side-panel-only controls do not satisfy this invariant. Every form shape and permission option emitted by a supported adapter must be covered by a finite wire-to-UI regression matrix.\\n+\\n+Actionability comes only from the live runtime's `pendingElicitations` and\\n+`pendingPermissions`; an open worker-stream row proves only that a request\\n+existed. After reattach or recreation, compare the durable open rows with the\\n+runtime-owned request ids and append terminal `cancelled` rows for any orphaned\\n+requests before publishing the recovered worker. The UI may render the stream\\n+rows as history, but must never turn them back into response controls.\\n*** Add File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-04-runtime-owns-actionable-human-input.md\\n+# The Runtime Owns Actionable Human Input\\n+\\n+**Date:** 2026-08-04\\n+**Context:** OmniHarness worker recovery, ACP elicitation and permission controls, unified worker stream\\n+**Symptom:** After a runner restart, the conversation showed two nearly identical question forms. The user could answer both, but the form created before the crash no longer had a live worker promise to receive its answer.\\n+**Root Cause:** The UI merged the runtime's live pending requests with historical `pending` rows from the append-only worker stream. Recovery restored the provider session but did not close stream requests that the new runtime instance no longer owned. A durable record of a past request was therefore mistaken for proof that the request was still actionable.\\n+**Fix:** Recovery and every worker reattach/recreate path now compare open stream requests with the runtime's authoritative pending request ids, append terminal `cancelled` entries for orphans, and emit `worker.human_input_reconciled`. Conversation and worker-card forms now render only runtime-confirmed requests.\\n+**Verification:** The regression was observed failing first in `tests/server/runs/recovery-reconciler.test.ts` and `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/worker-reattach.test.ts`, then passing after the fix. The UI ownership guard is covered by `tests/ui/conversation-actions.test.ts`.\\n+**Prevention:** Treat the worker stream as append-only history, not current ownership. Any response control must be backed by a live runtime pending request. Every recovery boundary must reconcile historical open requests before announcing that the worker is recovered.\\n+**Skill/Doc Updates:** Updated `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` with the actionability ownership rule. /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md\\n@@\\n ## Interrupted-turn recovery invariant\\n \\n Reattaching a provider session restores conversation context; it does not prove\\n@@\\n task so snapshot bootstrap does not block on the agent turn. A newer user turn\\n may supersede it, but that decision must resolve the recovery incident and emit\\n a named event.\\n+\\n+An intentional stop or steer abort is a successful ownership handoff, not a\\n+worker failure. Older initial-turn handlers must recognize the abort and must\\n+not emit `run_failed` or overwrite the replacement turn's status. Every queue\\n+row claimed by the interrupted delivery must leave `delivering`: close it as\\n+delivered if its exact message id reached the transcript, otherwise return it\\n+to pending. An elicitation answer resumes the turn that already owns the live\\n+question, so it must never wait behind that same turn's per-worker gate; doing\\n+so is a self-deadlock because the turn cannot finish until the answer arrives.\\n*** Add File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-04-interrupt-handoffs-must-not-fail-or-self-deadlock.md\\n+# Interrupt Handoffs Must Not Fail or Self-Deadlock\\n+\\n+**Date:** 2026-08-04\\n+**Context:** OmniHarness direct conversations, queued messages, live elicitation answers, and user steer recovery\\n+**Symptom:** Session `8d81f64ca066` showed “asking for your input,” then stayed “working” after the user sent `yes fix`. The run was actually failed, two queue rows were stuck in `delivering`, and the UI treated those orphaned rows as ongoing work.\\n+**Root Cause:** The queued answer to a live elicitation tried to acquire the per-worker turn gate already held by the turn waiting for that answer, creating a self-deadlock. The later steer correctly aborted the old turn, but both initial-turn catch handlers classified the intentional `WorkerTurnAbortedError` as a crash and emitted `run_failed`. A second abort path returned without releasing its claimed queue row.\\n+**Fix:** Deliver live elicitation answers directly to their owning turn instead of queueing behind it; classify intentional initial-turn aborts as expected handoffs; and always close or requeue a delivery row when a newer steer aborts or supersedes it. The affected session was resumed from its saved Claude session, its already-delivered `yes fix` row was reconciled by exact message id, and the stale recovery incident was resolved without redelivering the reply.\\n+**Verification:** Red-green regressions passed in `tests/server/conversations/create-steer.test.ts`, `tests/server/queued-message-interrupt.test.ts`, and `tests/server/queued-messages.test.ts` (24 tests total). The headless lifecycle scenario `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/direct-initial-steer.test.ts` passed in isolation. `pnpm typecheck` and `git diff --check` completed successfully.\\n+**Prevention:** Model stop/steer as an ownership transfer with explicit terminal handling for the old owner. Never acquire a resource gate from work that is already waiting inside that gate. Require every claimed queue state to have a terminal or retry transition on success, failure, abort, supersede, and restart.\\n+**Skill/Doc Updates:** Updated `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` with the intentional-abort, queue-release, and live-elicitation gate invariants. No global skill change was needed because the existing control-plane and client/server-state skills already require explicit ownership, terminal states, and observable decisions.\\n*** End Patch\";\n*** Update File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md\\n@@\\n snapshot bootstrap does not block on the agent turn.
